					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-subjectpageheader">
</div><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1320445320">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the line of scooters. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Vespa%22">"Vespa"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Vespa%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Vespa%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Vespa%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Vespa%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Vespa%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">May 2025</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Italian scooter</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1316064257">.mw-parser-output <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PerthVespa.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/PerthVespa.jpg/250px-PerthVespa.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/PerthVespa.jpg/500px-PerthVespa.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a><figcaption>Classic Vespas in <a href="/wiki/Perth" title="Perth">Perth</a>, Western Australia </figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><span><audio id="mwe_player_0" controls="" preload="none" data-mw-tmh="" class="mw-file-element" width="250" style="width:250px;" data-durationhint="44" data-mwtitle="Vespa.ogg" data-mwprovider="wikimediacommons" resource="/wiki/File:Vespa.ogg"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d4/Vespa.ogg" type="audio/ogg; codecs=&quot;vorbis&quot;" data-width="0" data-height="0" /><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/d/d4/Vespa.ogg/Vespa.ogg.mp3" type="audio/mpeg" data-transcodekey="mp3" data-width="0" data-height="0" /></audio></span><figcaption>Classic Vespa sound (43 seconds)</figcaption></figure>
<p><b>Vespa</b> (<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1177148991">.mw-parser-output .IPA-label-small{font-size:85%}.mw-parser-output .references .IPA-label-small,.mw-parser-output .infobox .IPA-label-small,.mw-parser-output .navbox .IPA-label-small{font-size:100%}</style><span class="IPA-label IPA-label-small">Italian pronunciation:</span> <span class="IPA nowrap" lang="it-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Italian" title="Help:IPA/Italian">&#91;ˈvɛspa&#93;</a></span>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a> for '<a href="/wiki/Wasp" title="Wasp">wasp</a>') is an Italian brand of <a href="/wiki/Scooter_(motorcycle)" title="Scooter (motorcycle)">scooters</a>  and <a href="/wiki/Moped" title="Moped">mopeds</a> manufactured by <a href="/wiki/Piaggio" title="Piaggio">Piaggio</a>. The Vespa has evolved from a single model motor scooter manufactured in 1946 by Piaggio &amp; Co. S.p.A. of <a href="/wiki/Pontedera" title="Pontedera">Pontedera</a>, Italy, to a full line of scooters and one of seven companies today owned by Piaggio.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-from&#95;Rider&#95;Magazine&#95;1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-from_Rider_Magazine-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>From their inception, Vespa scooters have been known for a painted, pressed steel body which combines, in a unified structure: a full cowling enclosure around the engine concealing dirt or grease, a flat floor panel protecting the feet, and a prominent front fairing to divert wind and rain.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vespamilitare.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Vespamilitare.jpg/250px-Vespamilitare.jpg" decoding="async" width="190" height="252" style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/32/Vespamilitare.jpg 2x" data-file-width="453" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Vespa_150_TAP" title="Vespa 150 TAP">Vespa 150 TAP</a>, modified for the French paratroopers that carried (not fired) an antitank weapon.</figcaption></figure>
<p>After World War II, in light of its agreement to cease war activities with the Allies, Italy had its aircraft industry severely restricted in both capability and capacity.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Piaggio emerged from the conflict with its <a href="/wiki/Pontedera" title="Pontedera">Pontedera</a> bomber plane plant demolished by bombing. Italy's crippled economy, and the disastrous state of its roads, were not immediately conducive to the redevelopment of the automobile market. <a href="/wiki/Enrico_Piaggio" title="Enrico Piaggio">Enrico Piaggio</a>, the son of Piaggio's founder Rinaldo Piaggio, decided to leave the aeronautical field to address Italy's urgent need for a modern and affordable mode of transportation for the masses.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 2024 <a href="/wiki/Piaggio" title="Piaggio">Piaggio</a> celebrated 140 years with limited edition of 'Vespa 140th of Piaggio,' with only 140 units available from 18 to 21 April 2024.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Design">Design</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Design"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Biella-MP5_Piaggio_Paperino.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Biella-MP5_Piaggio_Paperino.jpg/250px-Biella-MP5_Piaggio_Paperino.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Biella-MP5_Piaggio_Paperino.jpg/500px-Biella-MP5_Piaggio_Paperino.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a><figcaption>Piaggio MP5 "Paperino", the initial Piaggio prototype</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1944 Piaggio engineers Renzo Spolti and Vittorio Casini designed a motorcycle with bodywork fully enclosing the drivetrain and forming a tall splash guard at the front. In addition to the bodywork, the design included handlebar-mounted controls, forced air cooling, wheels of small diameter, and a tall central section that had to be straddled.  Officially known as the MP5 ("Moto Piaggio no. 5"), the prototype was nicknamed "Paperino" (meaning "Donald Duck" in Italian).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Vespa46&#95;06pp80&#95;83&#95;3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vespa46_06pp80_83-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
Piaggio was displeased with the MP5, especially the tall central section.  He contracted <a href="/wiki/Aerospace_engineering" title="Aerospace engineering">aeronautical engineer</a> <a href="/wiki/Corradino_D%27Ascanio" title="Corradino D&#39;Ascanio">Corradino D'Ascanio</a>, to redesign the scooter.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Vespa46&#95;06pp80&#95;83&#95;3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vespa46_06pp80_83-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>  D'Ascanio, who had earlier been consulted by <a href="/wiki/Ferdinando_Innocenti" title="Ferdinando Innocenti">Ferdinando Innocenti</a> about scooter design and manufacture, made it immediately known that he hated motorcycles, believing them to be bulky, dirty, and unreliable.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-RedEyes2022&#95;4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RedEyes2022-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>D'Ascanio's MP6 prototype had its engine mounted beside the rear wheel.  The wheel was driven directly from the transmission, eliminating the drive chain and the oil and dirt associated with it. The prototype had a unit spar frame with stress-bearing steel outer panels.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-RedEyes2022&#95;4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RedEyes2022-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>  These changes allowed the MP6 to have a step-through design instead of a tall centre section like that of the MP5 Paperino. The MP6 design also included a single-sided front suspension, interchangeable front and rear wheels mounted on stub axles, and a spare wheel. Other features of the MP6 were similar to those on the Paperino, including the handlebar-mounted controls and the enclosed bodywork with the tall front splash guard.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Vespa46&#95;06pp80&#95;83&#95;3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vespa46_06pp80_83-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Upon seeing the MP6 for the first time, Enrico Piaggio exclaimed: "Sembra una vespa!" ("It looks like a wasp!") Piaggio effectively named his new scooter on the spot.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-RedEyes2022&#95;4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RedEyes2022-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-thecarconnection.com&#95;5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thecarconnection.com-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <i>Vespa</i> is both <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> and Italian for wasp—derived from the vehicle's body shape: the thicker rear part connected to the front part by a narrow waist, and the steering rod resembled <a href="/wiki/Antenna_(biology)" class="mw-redirect" title="Antenna (biology)">antennae</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Product">Product</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Product"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:PepperBoxVespa.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/PepperBoxVespa.jpg/250px-PepperBoxVespa.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/PepperBoxVespa.jpg/500px-PepperBoxVespa.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a><figcaption>Glove box on newer <a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">Vespa PX</a> </figcaption></figure>
<p>On 23 April 1946 at 12 o'clock in the central office for inventions, models and makes of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Economic_Development_(Italy)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministry of Economic Development (Italy)">Ministry of Industry and Commerce</a> in <a href="/wiki/Florence" title="Florence">Florence</a>, Piaggio e C. S.p.A. took out a patent for a "motorcycle of a rational complexity of organs and elements combined with a frame with mudguards and a casing covering the whole mechanical part".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-VASOS&#95;6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VASOS-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The basic patented design allowed a series of features to be deployed on the spar-frame that would later allow quick development of new models. The original Vespa featured a rear <a href="/wiki/Pillion" title="Pillion">pillion</a> seat for a passenger, or optionally a storage compartment. The original front protection "shield" was a flat piece of aero metal; later, this developed into a twin skin to allow additional storage behind the front shield, similar to the glove compartment in a car. The fuel cap was located underneath the (hinged) seat, which saved the cost of an additional lock on the fuel cap or need for additional metal work on the smooth skin.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The scooter had rigid rear suspension and small 8-inch (200&#160;mm) wheels that allowed a compact design and plenty of room for the rider's legs. The Vespa's enclosed, horizontally mounted 98&#160;cc <a href="/wiki/Two-stroke_engine" title="Two-stroke engine">two-stroke engine</a> acted directly on the rear drive wheel through a three-speed transmission. The <a href="/wiki/Twistgrip" title="Twistgrip">twistgrip</a>-controlled gear change involved a system of rods. The early engine had no forced-air cooling, but fan blades were soon attached to the <a href="/wiki/Magneto" title="Magneto">magneto</a>-flywheel (which houses the points and generates electricity for accessories and for the engine's spark) to push air over the cylinder's cooling fins.  The modern Vespa engine is still cooled this way.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The MP6 prototype had large grilles on the front and rear of the rear fender covering the engine. This was done to allow air in to cool the engine, as the prototype did not have fan cooling.  A cooling fan similar to that used on the MP5 "Paperino" prototype was included in the design of the production Vespa, and the grilles were removed from the fender.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Vespa46&#95;06pp80&#95;83&#95;3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vespa46_06pp80_83-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Launch">Launch</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Launch"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305433154" /><table class="box-Unreferenced&#95;section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Vespa" title="Special:EditPage/Vespa">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">May 2025</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Piaggio filed a patent for the Vespa scooter design in April 1946. The application documents referred to a "model of a practical nature" for a "motorcycle with rationally placed parts and elements with a frame combining with mudguards and engine-cowling covering all working parts", of which "the whole constitutes a rational, comfortable motorcycle offering protection from mud and dust without jeopardizing requirements of appearance and elegance". The patent was approved the following December.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The first 13 examples appeared in spring 1946, and revealed their aeronautical background. In the first examples, one can recognize the typical aircraft technology. Attention to aerodynamics is evident in all the design, in particular on the tail. It was also one of the first vehicles to use monocoque construction (where the body is an integral part of the chassis).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The company was aiming to manufacture the new Vespa in large numbers, and their longstanding industrial experience led to an efficient <a href="/wiki/Ford_Motor_Company" title="Ford Motor Company">Ford-style</a> volume production line. The scooter was presented to the press at Rome Golf Club, where journalists were apparently mystified by the strange, pastel coloured, toy-like object on display. However, the road tests were encouraging, and even with no rear suspension the machine was more manoeuvrable and comfortable to ride than a traditional motorcycle.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Following its public debut at the 1946 Milan Fair, the first fifty sold slowly.  With the introduction of payment by installments, sales took off.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Sales_and_development">Sales and development</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Sales and development"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vespasidecar.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Vespasidecar.JPG/250px-Vespasidecar.JPG" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Vespasidecar.JPG/500px-Vespasidecar.JPG 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a><figcaption>Original Vespa with attached sidecar</figcaption></figure>
<p>Piaggio sold some 2,500 Vespas in 1947, over 10,000 in 1948, 20,000 in 1949, and over 60,000 in 1950.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-PatrickTaylorVespa&#95;7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PatrickTaylorVespa-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The biggest sales promotion ever came courtesy of <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_the_United_States" title="Cinema of the United States">Hollywood</a>. In 1952, <a href="/wiki/Audrey_Hepburn" title="Audrey Hepburn">Audrey Hepburn</a> side-saddled <a href="/wiki/Gregory_Peck" title="Gregory Peck">Gregory Peck</a>'s Vespa in the feature film <i><a href="/wiki/Roman_Holiday_(1953_film)" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Holiday (1953 film)">Roman Holiday</a></i> for a ride through Rome, resulting in over 100,000 sales. In 1956, <a href="/wiki/John_Wayne" title="John Wayne">John Wayne</a> dismounted from his horse in favor of the two-wheeler to get between takes on sets,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-ScooteringUSA&#95;8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ScooteringUSA-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Marlon_Brando" title="Marlon Brando">Marlon Brando</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dean_Martin" title="Dean Martin">Dean Martin</a>, and the entertainer <a href="/wiki/Abbe_Lane" title="Abbe Lane">Abbe Lane</a> had become Vespa owners. <a href="/wiki/William_Wyler" title="William Wyler">William Wyler</a> filmed <i><a href="/wiki/Ben-Hur_(1959_film)" title="Ben-Hur (1959 film)">Ben Hur</a></i> in Rome in 1959, allowing <a href="/wiki/Charlton_Heston" title="Charlton Heston">Charlton Heston</a> to abandon horse and chariot between takes to take a spin on the Vespa.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-DolceVita&#95;9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DolceVita-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Capital&#95;10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Capital-10"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>10<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Vespa clubs popped up throughout Europe, and by 1952, worldwide Vespa Club membership had surpassed 50,000. By the mid-1950s, Vespas were being manufactured under licence in Germany, the United Kingdom, France, Belgium and Spain; in the 1960s, production was started in India, Brazil and Indonesia. By 1956, one million had been sold, then two million by 1960. By the 1960s, the Vespa&#8212;originally conceived as a utility vehicle&#8212;had come to symbolize freedom and imagination, and resulted in further sales boosts: four million by 1970, and ten million by the late 1980s.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Improvements were made to the original design and new models were introduced. The 1948 Vespa 125 had rear suspension and a bigger engine. The headlamp was moved up to the handlebars in 1953 and had more engine power and a restyled rear fairing. A cheaper spartan version was also available. One of the best-loved models was the Vespa 150 GS introduced in 1955 with a 150&#160;cc engine, a long saddle, and the faired handlebar-headlamp unit. Then came the 50&#160;cc of 1963, and in 1968 Vespa 125 Primavera became one of the most durable of all.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Piaggio_vespa_px125_060117.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Piaggio_vespa_px125_060117.jpg/250px-Piaggio_vespa_px125_060117.jpg" decoding="async" width="190" height="254" style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Piaggio_vespa_px125_060117.jpg/500px-Piaggio_vespa_px125_060117.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1952" data-file-height="2608" /></a><figcaption>T5 Millennium from the <a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">PX</a> series</figcaption></figure>
<p>Vespas came in two sizes, referred to as "largeframe" and "smallframe". The smallframe scooters came in 50&#160;cc, 90&#160;cc, 100&#160;cc, and 125&#160;cc versions, all using an engine derived from the 50&#160;cc model of 1963, and the largeframe scooters in 125&#160;cc, 150&#160;cc, 160&#160;cc, 180&#160;cc, and 200&#160;cc displacements using engines derived from the redesigned 125&#160;cc VNA engine from 1957.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The largeframe Vespa evolved into the <a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">PX</a> range in the late 1970s and was produced in 125, 150 and 200&#160;cc versions until July 2007. Starting in 1981, an 80cc version was available as well. Piaggio reintroduced the PX 125 and 150 models in 2011, with a revised EURO3 compliant engine.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The smallframe evolved into the PK range in the early 1980s, although some vintage-styled smallframes were produced for the Japanese market as late as the mid-1990s.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1950s_and_beyond">1950s and beyond</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 1950s and beyond"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The ET model range stuck true to the wasp/aero design principles. It was lighter, more aerodynamic, had an automatic gearbox and could take a series of engines from a 50&#160;cc in either <a href="/wiki/Two-stroke_cycle" class="mw-redirect" title="Two-stroke cycle">two-stroke</a> or <a href="/wiki/Four-stroke_cycle" class="mw-redirect" title="Four-stroke cycle">four-stroke</a>, up to a 150&#160;cc four stroke.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-ET2Analysis&#95;11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ET2Analysis-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Under_new_ownership">Under new ownership</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Under new ownership"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305433154" /><table class="box-Unreferenced&#95;section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Vespa" title="Special:EditPage/Vespa">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">July 2010</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>In 1959 Piaggio came under the control of the <a href="/wiki/Agnelli_family" title="Agnelli family">Agnelli family</a>, the owners of car maker <a href="/wiki/Fiat_S.p.A." title="Fiat S.p.A.">Fiat S.p.A.</a> Vespa thrived until 1992 when <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Alberto_Agnelli" title="Giovanni Alberto Agnelli">Giovanni Alberto Agnelli</a> became CEO, but Agnelli was already suffering from cancer and died in 1997. In 1999 <a href="/wiki/Morgan_Grenfell" class="mw-redirect" title="Morgan Grenfell">Morgan Grenfell Private Equity</a> acquired Piaggio, but a quickly hoped-for sale was dashed by a failed joint venture in China.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>By 2003 the company found itself close to bankruptcy. Continual management changes and great sums spent on many different plans and products had saddled Piaggio with debt and left it vulnerable to competition from cheaper Asian rivals. Despite this, the brand was still well-known and products like the Vespa ET4 were gaining positive publicity. In October 2003 <a href="/wiki/Roberto_Colaninno" title="Roberto Colaninno">Roberto Colaninno</a> made an initial investment of €100&#160;million through his holding company Immsi S.p.A. in exchange for just under a third of Piaggio and the mandate to run it. Chief executive Rocco Sabelli redesigned the factory to Japanese principles so that every Piaggio scooter could be made on any assembly line.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In 2004 the company introduced a gas-electric hybrid scooter and a scooter with two wheels at the front and one at the back.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Piaggio acquired scooter and motorcycle maker <a href="/wiki/Aprilia" title="Aprilia">Aprilia</a> in 2006 and in that same year Piaggio shares were launched onto the <a href="/wiki/Borsa_Italiana" title="Borsa Italiana">Borsa Italiana</a> with the listing symbol PIAGF.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Relaunch_in_North_America">Relaunch in North America</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Relaunch in North America"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305433154" /><table class="box-Unreferenced&#95;section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Vespa" title="Special:EditPage/Vespa">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">July 2010</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Piaggio first came back into the market in 2001 with the ET2 (two stroke 50&#160;cc) and ET4 (four stroke 150&#160;cc). In 2004, the PX (model year 2005) was reintroduced to North America to meet market demand for the classic Vespa design. Growth in the US market and worldwide environmental concerns meant a need for larger and cleaner engines, so Vespa developed the LEADER (Low Emissions ADvanced Engine Range) series of four-stroke engines. The larger Granturismo frame, with larger 12-inch (300&#160;mm) wheels, was introduced to handle the additional power. The bike in 2006 spawned the iconic GTS-250ie version, with an upgraded suspension and the new QUASAR (QUArter-liter Smooth Augmented Range) 250&#160;cc fuel-injected engine, capable of 80+ mph.  As of the end of 2010 the GTS 250 has been replaced by the GTS 300 which has a 278cc fuel – injected engine. In 2005, the ET was withdrawn from Europe and North America and replaced by a new small-frame scooter, the LX range. These were available in the US in 50&#160;cc and 150&#160;cc versions, while Europeans could choose a 50 cc, 125&#160;cc and 150&#160;cc..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Design_and_Culture_Icon">Design and Culture Icon</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Design and Culture Icon"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:VespaMod.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/VespaMod.jpg/250px-VespaMod.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="296" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/VespaMod.jpg/500px-VespaMod.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2238" data-file-height="2652" /></a><figcaption>Modified Vespa as popular in the <a href="/wiki/Mod_(subculture)" title="Mod (subculture)">mods</a>/<a href="/wiki/Skinhead" title="Skinhead">skinhead</a> culture </figcaption></figure>
<p>In recent years, many urban commuters have purchased new or restored Vespas.  A shortage of available parking for automobiles in large urban areas and the Vespa's low running costs are two reasons for the increase in Vespa (and other scooter) popularity. The cultural use of the scooter as a recreational vehicle with a sub-cultural following in the US, Canada and parts of Europe and Japan has also contributed to the rise in Vespa ownership. In contrast, the Vespa is considered a utilitarian vehicle for hauling products and sometimes up to 5 family members in much of Asia and Mexico.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>
In the 2000s Indonesia, classic Vespa culture was born as people treat Vespa as hobby, recreational, and historic vehicle, although many still use them as regular transportation. In this era, very low priced and abundance stocks of used Vespa models from 1970s-1990s leads to people using Vespa as a form of modification to express their freedom and rebel. Eventually, the "<b>Vespa Extreme</b>" culture was born in this era, where Vespa users heavily even extremely customizing their vespa until it really have completely different form compared the original shape of standard Vespa scooters, in respect to values such as freedom, rebellion, and unestablished-ness.</p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Indonesian_Vespa_Extreme.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Indonesian_Vespa_Extreme.jpg/250px-Indonesian_Vespa_Extreme.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="164" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Indonesian_Vespa_Extreme.jpg/500px-Indonesian_Vespa_Extreme.jpg 2x" data-file-width="685" data-file-height="448" /></a><figcaption>Vespa Extreme member and their ride</figcaption></figure><p>
Since 2010s until now, classic Vespas continued to have a large group of enthusiasts across Indonesia who kept vintage scooters on the road by rebuilding, restoring, and adding performance-enhancing engine parts as the stock parts would wear out. Vintage Vespas also saw resurgence in price as people see more historic value in the classic Vespa scooters, especially one with original condition (paint, body part, engine, etc.)</p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mods_May_Day_Parade_Jakarta_2025.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Mods_May_Day_Parade_Jakarta_2025.jpg/250px-Mods_May_Day_Parade_Jakarta_2025.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="333" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Mods_May_Day_Parade_Jakarta_2025.jpg/500px-Mods_May_Day_Parade_Jakarta_2025.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4284" data-file-height="5712" /></a><figcaption>Vespa Riders Strolls Around The Street of Jakarta in The Jakarta Mods May Day 2025 Parade</figcaption></figure>
<p>This resurgence in interest in vintage motor scooters has also spawned a scooter restoration industry, with many restored Vespas being exported from Thailand, Vietnam and Indonesia to the rest of the world.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-HowBad&#95;13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HowBad-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-ChaoVespa&#95;14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ChaoVespa-14"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>14<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Vierra&#95;15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vierra-15"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>There is a Piaggio Museum &amp; Gift Shop adjacent to the plant in central Pontedera, near Pisa, Tuscany. The permanent exhibition includes those items which toured  venues such as the Guggenheim in New York and the Centre Pompidou in Paris. Also on display is a model personally customised by <a href="/wiki/Salvador_Dal%C3%AD" title="Salvador Dalí">Salvador Dalí</a> in 1962..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Miami_Auto_Museum" class="mw-redirect" title="Miami Auto Museum">Miami Auto Museum</a> in <a href="/wiki/North_Miami,_Florida" title="North Miami, Florida">North Miami, Florida</a>, claims to have largest collection of Vespa scooters with over 400 items.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>16<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Global_markets">Global markets</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Global markets"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Europe">Europe</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Europe"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Vespa's largest market by all measures globally is still Italy, but as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Mod_(subculture)" title="Mod (subculture)">Mod subculture</a> that developed in the 1960s, the United Kingdom is still Vespa's second largest global market—and at one point in the 1960s, its largest. The appeal of the Vespa to the style-conscious mods was the weather protection. Their counterparts, the <a href="/wiki/Rocker_(subculture)" title="Rocker (subculture)">rockers</a> rode classic British motorcycles such as <a href="/wiki/Triumph_Bonneville" title="Triumph Bonneville">Triumph Bonneville</a> and <a href="/wiki/Birmingham_Small_Arms_Company" title="Birmingham Small Arms Company">BSAs</a>, and needed to wear leathers against the elements. Mods would modify their Vespas, adding lights, mascots, accessories, various racks and crash bars. A new lifestyle evolved in the UK, with thousands attending <a href="/w/index.php?title=Scooter_rally&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Scooter rally (page does not exist)">scooter rallies</a>..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The dominance of the Vespa declined through the 1970s, as small car ownership increased and cheap and reliable commuter bikes like the <a href="/wiki/Honda_Super_Cub" title="Honda Super Cub">Honda Super Cub</a> hit sales. Despite the introduction of the more modern <a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">'P' range</a> in the 1970s, the lack of development cost Vespa, and like other markets, the sales fell off drastically in the economic boom of the 1980s. Then Vespa introduced the trendy automatic ET2, the city of London introduced the <a href="/wiki/London_congestion_charge" title="London congestion charge">congestion charge</a> and—partly with celebrity chef <a href="/wiki/Jamie_Oliver" title="Jamie Oliver">Jamie Oliver</a>'s indirect help from his <a href="/wiki/BBC2" class="mw-redirect" title="BBC2">BBC2</a> TV series—sales suddenly leapt.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>17<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Indonesia">Indonesia</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Indonesia"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Indonesia have imported Vespa Scooters since the early 1950s, but not until 1972 Indonesia was able to built their own Vespa through PT Danmotors Vespa Indonesia (DMVI).
</p><p>In the Mid-1970s, Danmotors reached their peak sales performance for the Vespa Scooters, making Indonesia the second-largest global market for Vespa aside from Italy itself. Sales performance continued to rise through the 1980s and finally began to fall in the 1990s as more modern and cheaper Japanese manufactured motorcycle gained favor among customers.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="North_America">North America</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: North America"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305433154" /><table class="box-More&#95;citations&#95;needed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Vespa" title="Special:EditPage/Vespa">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>&#32;in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Vespa%22">"Vespa"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Vespa%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Vespa%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Vespa%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Vespa%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Vespa%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vespadealership.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Vespadealership.jpg/250px-Vespadealership.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="201" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Vespadealership.jpg/500px-Vespadealership.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="802" /></a><figcaption>A Vespa Boutique in San Francisco</figcaption></figure>
<p>Much as Vespa had used the Cushman Army scooter as inspiration for its original design, Vespa in turn made scooters for <a href="/wiki/Sears" title="Sears">Sears, Roebuck &amp; Company</a> (labeled and marketed as "Allstate" scooters) and <a href="/wiki/Cushman_(company)" title="Cushman (company)">Cushman</a> after World War II.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>18<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Imported by Morton Colby of Colby General Tire Company, 662 East Fordham Road, Bronx, New York, the Sears models were 3- and 4-speed 125&#160;cc Vespas rebadged as Sears Allstate <a href="/w/index.php?title=Cruiseaire&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cruiseaire (page does not exist)">Cruiseaires</a>. Innocenti also distributed their <a href="/wiki/Lambretta" title="Lambretta">Lambretta</a> brand via <a href="/wiki/Montgomery_Ward" title="Montgomery Ward">Montgomery Ward</a>'s catalogue during this post-World War II period. These were the premier brands of scooters, bringing premium pricing to many, including farmers, whose link to the outside world was via purchases made in these catalogues. Cushman sold rebadged Vespa scooters as Cushmans, but many Cushman dealers refused to market a "foreign" machine.  However, collectors prize the Cushman Vespa because it is relatively rare..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Two expensive product-liability lawsuits, increased competition from Japanese scooters, and certain states' passing so-called "green laws" led to the bankruptcy of Vespa's American importer and the withdrawal of Vespa from the US market in late 1981..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>During 1981–2001, despite an absence of United States domestic sales, Vespas continued to have a core group of enthusiasts who kept vintage scooters on the road by rebuilding, restoring, and adding performance-enhancing engine parts as the stock parts would wear out..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Vespa returned to the American market in 2001 with a new, more modern style ET series, in 50&#160;cc two and four stroke, and 150&#160;cc four-stroke. "These adjustments in engine power and the production of high-end “touring" Vespas make it apt for long-distance driving."<sup id="cite&#95;ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> According to the Motorcycle Industry Council, U.S. scooter sales increased fivefold over six years, swelling from 12,000 units in 1997 to 69,000 units in 2002. Vespa sales in the U.S. increased 27 percent between 2001 and 2002. The 65 "Vespa Boutiques" scattered throughout the U.S. gave scooterists a place to buy, service, and customize Vespa scooters, and outfit themselves in everything from Vespa watches and helmets to Vespa jackets, T-shirts, and sunglasses. Vespa restarted its American sales effort, opening its first boutique on Ventura Boulevard in <a href="/wiki/Sherman_Oaks,_California" class="mw-redirect" title="Sherman Oaks, California">Sherman Oaks, California</a>..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In light of vastly-increasing US sales, Vespa developed the GT, offered as a 200&#160;cc four-stroke and a 125&#160;cc variant in Europe. In 2004, Vespa reintroduced a modernized PX 150 to the United States. In the fall of 2005, Piaggio offered their largest-selling Vespa scooter ever, the 250&#160;cc-engined GTS250, available in Europe with ABS.  In 2009, Vespa released the GTS 300 which can cruise at 105–113&#160;km/h (65–70&#160;mph).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-GTS300SuperReview&#95;20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GTS300SuperReview-20"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>20<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Asia/Pacific"><span id="Asia.2FPacific"></span>Asia/Pacific</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Asia/Pacific"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Vespa have exported Scooters to Australia since the 1960s. They have recently started exporting to India for the first time (traditionally, that market was served by licensed Indian versions of the Vespa made by LML and Bajaj). Vespa produces some of its scooters in Vietnam, and has also sold its Italian ones there.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>21<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Production_outside_Italy">Production outside Italy</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Production outside Italy"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BKK_Vespa078.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/BKK_Vespa078.jpg/250px-BKK_Vespa078.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/BKK_Vespa078.jpg/500px-BKK_Vespa078.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Bangkok" title="Bangkok">Bangkok</a>: Vespa in use to haul cargo </figcaption></figure>
<p>Vespa scooters were sold beyond Europe and North America.  When expanding into these markets it was common for Vespa to partner with, or license certain models to, existing manufacturers..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="India">India</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: India"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Piaggio first licensed the production of Vespa scooters in India to <a href="/wiki/Bajaj_Auto" title="Bajaj Auto">Bajaj Auto</a> in the 1960s. In 1971, Piaggio's license was not renewed as a part of then Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>'s changes to foreign ownership rules, which favoured Indian control over foreign brands. When the licence expired, Bajaj lost the right to use the Vespa name, but not the ability to manufacture scooters using the knowledge and tooling it had already lawfully acquired, and Bajaj continued to produce scooters based on the Vespa design, namely the <a href="/wiki/Bajaj_Chetak" title="Bajaj Chetak">Chetak</a>.
<sup id="cite&#95;ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>22<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> 
</p><p>Another Vespa partner in India was that of <a href="/wiki/Lohia_Machinery_Limited" title="Lohia Machinery Limited">LML Motors</a>.  Beginning as a joint-venture with Piaggio in 1983, LML, in addition to being a large parts supplier for Piaggio, produced the <a href="/wiki/LML_NV" class="mw-redirect" title="LML NV">P-Series scooters for the Indian market</a>. In 1999, after protracted dispute with Piaggio, LML bought back Piaggio's stake in the company and the partnership ceased.  LML continued to produce (and also exported) the P-Series variant known as the <a href="/wiki/Stella_(scooter)" class="mw-redirect" title="Stella (scooter)">Stella</a> in the U.S. market and by other names in different markets.  LML Factory which produced these P-Series variants issued a notice of insolvency on 2 June 2017 and closed down permanently in 2018.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the 2012 Auto Expo held in New Delhi, the iconic Vespa re-entered the Indian market. Piaggio unveiled its range of scooters at the Expo. This became the first such venture of Piaggio in India without a local partner.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-PiaggioInIndia&#95;23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PiaggioInIndia-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Indonesia_2">Indonesia</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Indonesia"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>PT Danmotors Vespa Indonesia (DMVI) was a joint venture between Indonesian interests and the East Asiatic Company, which was based in Denmark.  Between 1972 and 2001, it produced Vespas under license for the Indonesian market.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-EastAsiaticCo&#95;24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EastAsiaticCo-24"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>24<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-MasihBerbatuBatu&#95;25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MasihBerbatuBatu-25"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>25<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
In 1976 approximately 40,000 units were produced giving DMVI the third biggest share of the Indonesian scooter market.  Government tax incentives allowed these scooters to be exported to Thailand at less than the domestic market price, so that they would be economically competitive.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>26<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>From 1972 to 1977, DMVI only built 90 and 150cc models, while 100cc, 150cc, and 200cc model were built in the 1980s.  
</p><p>From 1972 onwards the company was located at a purpose-built factory in <a href="/wiki/Pulo_Gadung" title="Pulo Gadung">Pulo Gadung</a>.  This was greatly expanded in 1977 to also manufacture sub-components, following a government decree that a higher domestically built proportion of these should be used.  Sub-components were also bought from other Indonesian manufacturers after their quality had been approved by Piaggio.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Odaka&#95;27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Odaka-27"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>27<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In early 2000s, the company changed its name to PT Danmotor Indonesia.
</p><p>Production from PT Danmotor Indonesia (DMVI) ceased in early 2000s as the company was impacted from 1998 Asian monetary crisis and the surge of Japanese-manufactured mopeds. The last classic 2-stroke Vespa built by the company was approximately sold in 2007.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Taiwan">Taiwan</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Taiwan"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Vespa has had various partnerships and presence in Taiwan. In 1965, Taiwan Vespa Co. Ltd was licensed for Vespa scooter production. From 1972 to 1982, Vespa entered into a collaboration with scooter manufacturer <a href="/wiki/PGO_Scooters" title="PGO Scooters">PGO</a>. In 1978, Vespa entered into a collaboration with <a href="/wiki/Taiwan_Golden_Bee" title="Taiwan Golden Bee">TGB</a>, which to some extent, continues to this day (namely with CVT transmission production)..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="United_Kingdom">United Kingdom</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: United Kingdom"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>In 1951 the financially troubled motorcycle manufacturer <a href="/wiki/Douglas_(motorcycles)" title="Douglas (motorcycles)">Douglas</a> started production of Piaggio licensed Vespa scooters in <a href="/wiki/Bristol,_England" class="mw-redirect" title="Bristol, England">Bristol</a>, with a market to include some <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth countries</a> as well as the UK. It was a year which also saw the first meeting of the newly-formed Vespa scooter clubs with nearly 20,000 riders showing up for the event.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>28<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Westinghouse_Brake_and_Signal_Company_Ltd" class="mw-redirect" title="Westinghouse Brake and Signal Company Ltd">Westinghouse</a> took over Douglas in 1955, and while motorcycle production was stopped in 1957, Vespa production in Britain continued until 1965.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-veteranvespaclub.com&#95;29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-veteranvespaclub.com-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Douglas was more than a simple assembly plant for parts imported from Piaggio; many of the models made by Douglas had a high percentage of content manufactured by Douglas (<i>eg cylinder heads, gear clusters, brake drums and other mechanical parts</i>) or by UK supply companies (<i>eg seats, carburetors, tyres, and some electrical components were made in the UK</i>). Models made by Douglas were primarily the Vespa 125 and 150 (VBB).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-veteranvespaclub.com&#95;29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-veteranvespaclub.com-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Douglas produced Vespas which were often outdated compared to current Italian models. Douglas failed to meet the production numbers hoped for by Piaggio, though after production ceased in 1965, Douglas remained the UK importer for Vespa scooters until its demise in 1982.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-veteranvespaclub.com&#95;29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-veteranvespaclub.com-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Spain">Spain</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Spain"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>In 1953 a factory in Madrid began production of <a href="/wiki/Motovespa" class="mw-redirect" title="Motovespa">Motovespas</a>, production continued until 2000. The factory was demolished in 2001..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Soviet_Union">Soviet Union</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Soviet Union"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Between 1956 and 1966 an unauthorised, <a href="/wiki/Reverse-engineered" class="mw-redirect" title="Reverse-engineered">reverse-engineered</a> version of the Vespa was built and sold in the USSR by <a href="/wiki/Vyatka_(motor_scooter)" title="Vyatka (motor scooter)">Vyatka</a>.  This was withdrawn after protests by Piaggio.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>30<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Racing">Racing</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Racing"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>In the 1950s and early 1960s Vespa and Lambretta scooters were raced competitively against motorcycles, often winning the races. In the mid-1960s, motorcycle engines became larger and faster, and a gap was created—along with varying cc classifications. Since the 1980s, Vespa and Lambretta racing has grown into a serious sport in the United States. There are various classes in the United States, depending on the racing association. They are generally:
</p>
<ul><li>Small Frame Class: Open class up to 152&#160;cc</li>
<li>Automatics Class</li>
<li>Specials Class</li></ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Models">Models</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Models"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>There have been many different versions of the Vespa.  Today five series are in production: the <a href="/wiki/Continuously_variable_transmission" title="Continuously variable transmission">CVT transmission</a> Sprint, Primavera, GTS, GTV, Elettrica, and 946..<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>

</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">PX 150 Classic</a> (1993-96)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">PX 150 Disco</a> (1997-99)</li>

<li><a href="/wiki/Vespa_PX" title="Vespa PX">PX 150</a> (2011-14)</li></ul>
<p>in 2011 the PX series restarted to be produced in Italy after a 3-year absence because of the European Union restriction about <a href="/wiki/Euro_III" class="mw-redirect" title="Euro III">Euro III</a> engines emissions not followed. In occasion of the 150th anniversary of Italian union, Piaggio has proposed this special version, with a re-designed saddle but with the same "Vespa experience".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>32<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Vespa_946">Vespa 946</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Vespa 946"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Vespa_946" title="Vespa 946">Vespa 946</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vespa_946_schraeg.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Vespa_946_schraeg.jpg/250px-Vespa_946_schraeg.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Vespa_946_schraeg.jpg/500px-Vespa_946_schraeg.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4752" data-file-height="3168" /></a><figcaption>Vespa 946</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <b>Vespa 946</b> is a scooter announced by Piaggio, sold under their Vespa brand, that sold starting in July 2013. Piaggio presented the <a href="/wiki/Retro-futurism" class="mw-redirect" title="Retro-futurism">retro-futurist</a> Vespa Quarantasei <a href="/wiki/Concept_vehicle" class="mw-redirect" title="Concept vehicle">concept</a>, based on the 1945 Vespa MP6 prototype, at the 2011 <a href="/wiki/EICMA" title="EICMA">EICMA</a> motorcycle show. The final production version, renamed the Vespa 946, appeared the following year, at EICMA 2012. The 946 will be fitted with Piaggio's new <a href="/wiki/Air-cooled_engine" title="Air-cooled engine">air-cooled</a>, <a href="/wiki/Multi-valve" title="Multi-valve">three-valve</a>, <a href="/wiki/Single-cylinder_engine" title="Single-cylinder engine">single-cylinder engine</a>, with a claimed output of 11.7&#160;hp (8.7&#160;kW) for the 125&#160;cc (7.6&#160;cu&#160;in) <a href="/wiki/Engine_displacement" title="Engine displacement">displacement</a> version, and 13&#160;hp (9.7&#160;kW) for the 150&#160;cc (9.2&#160;cu&#160;in) version.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Harley2011&#95;35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Harley2011-35"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>35<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-dePrato2012&#95;36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dePrato2012-36"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>36<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Wilson2012&#95;37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wilson2012-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Electric_vehicles">Electric vehicles</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Electric vehicles"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vespa-Elettrica-Madrid-070219.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Vespa-Elettrica-Madrid-070219.jpg/250px-Vespa-Elettrica-Madrid-070219.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Vespa-Elettrica-Madrid-070219.jpg/500px-Vespa-Elettrica-Madrid-070219.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a><figcaption>Electric Vespa Electrica in Madrid, Spain</figcaption></figure>
<p>Vespa Elettrica with a 2&#160;kWh battery  and a range of 100&#160;km (62&#160;mi).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>38<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> There will be an hybrid option as well with a range of 200&#160;km. Piaggio started production in September 2018 and it was released at the beginning of 2019.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>39<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:left;" class=""></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Hybrids">Hybrids</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Hybrids"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Piaggio/Vespa is developing a range of hybrid scooters. Two models are being developed at present, based on the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa_LX_50&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Vespa LX 50 (page does not exist)">Vespa LX 50</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Piaggio_X8" title="Piaggio X8">Piaggio X8</a> 125.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Specials">Specials</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vespa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Specials"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>One-offs and special machines:
</p>
<ul><li>Montlhéry – produced in 1950 to break world records on the French <a href="/wiki/Montlh%C3%A9ry" title="Montlhéry">circuit of the same name</a>. It smashed 17 records in 10 hours</li>
<li>Torpedo – 1951 125&#160;cc special with counter-opposing pistons. <a href="/w/index.php?title=Dino_Mazzoncini&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Dino Mazzoncini (page does not exist)">Dino Mazzoncini</a> set the world record on the kilometre at an average of 171&#160;km/h</li></ul>
